[Coordinates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system "Geographic coordinate system"): ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/WMA_button2b.png/20px-WMA_button2b.png)[6°27′22″N3°23′37″E﻿ / ﻿6.45611°N 3.39361°E﻿ / 6.45611; 3.39361](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Lagos&params=6_27_22_N_3_23_37_E_region:NG-LA_type:city)

From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

Most populous city in Nigeria

This article is about the city, officially known as Lagos Metropolitan Area. For other uses, see [Lagos (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_(disambiguation) "Lagos (disambiguation)").

"Èkó" redirects here. For the song, see [Èkó (song)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%88k%C3%B3_(song) "Èkó (song)").

Not to be confused with [Legos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lego_(disambiguation) "Lego (disambiguation)"); [Laos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laos "Laos"); [Lagos, Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos,_Portugal "Lagos, Portugal"); or [Lagos State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State "Lagos State").

**Lagos** ([Pronunciation](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/6a/Ta-Lagos.ogg/Ta-Lagos.ogg.mp3 "Play audio")[ⓘ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ta-Lagos.ogg "File:Ta-Lagos.ogg"); [/ˈleɪɡɒs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [_LAY-goss_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key");[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-:14)[&#8202;](#ref-back-m-w-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-m-w)[&#8202;](#ref-back-15-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-15) [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language "Yoruba language"): _Èkó_[\[èkó\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Yoruba "Help:IPA/Yoruba")), or **Lagos City**, is a large metropolitan port city in southwestern [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria"). As of November 2025[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit), the size of the city's population has been estimated to be between 17 and 21 million residents, making Lagos the largest city in Nigeria, the most populous urban area on the African continent,[&#8202;](#ref-back-:16-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-:16)[&#8202;](#ref-back-17-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-17)[&#8202;](#ref-back-18-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-18)[&#8202;](#ref-back-19-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-19) and one of the fastest-growing [megacities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megacity "Megacity") in the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-20-0)[\[18\]](#refnote-20)[&#8202;](#ref-back-21-0)[\[19\]](#refnote-21)[&#8202;](#ref-back-29-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-29)[&#8202;](#ref-back-30-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-30)

The lands are separated by creeks, fringing the southwest mouth of [Lagos Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Lagoon "Lagos Lagoon"), while being protected from the [Atlantic Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean "Atlantic Ocean") by [barrier islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrier_island "Barrier island") and long sand spits such as [Bar Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Beach,_Lagos "Bar Beach, Lagos"), which stretch up to 100 kilometres (62mi) east and west of the mouth.

With its [international airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murtala_Muhammed_International_Airport "Murtala Muhammed International Airport"), three ports ( [Apapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apapa_Port_Complex "Apapa Port Complex"), Tin Can Island and [Lekki deep-sea port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Port "Lekki Port")), four expressways (to [Badagry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos%E2%80%93Badagry_Expressway "Lagos–Badagry Expressway"), [Abeokuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos%E2%80%93Abeokuta_Expressway "Lagos–Abeokuta Expressway"), [Ibadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos%E2%80%93Ibadan_Expressway "Lagos–Ibadan Expressway") and [Calabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos-Calabar_Coastal_Highway "Lagos-Calabar Coastal Highway"), the latter under construction since 2023 and partially operational in 2026), and the [standard-gauge rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard-gauge_railway "Standard-gauge railway") link[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-31)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lagos-Ibadan_train_Experience-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-Lagos-Ibadan_train_Experience) to [Abeokuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abeokuta "Abeokuta") and [Ibadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibadan "Ibadan"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-33-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-33)[&#8202;](#ref-back-34-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-34) the city is well connected in terms of transport and handles 88% of Nigeria’s exports.[&#8202;](#ref-back-35-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-35) The [urban rail transit system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Rail_Mass_Transit "Lagos Rail Mass Transit") in Lagos is regarded as one of the most modern of its kind in Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LAMATA_rail_service_expands_to_94_daily_trips-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-LAMATA_rail_service_expands_to_94_daily_trips)[&#8202;](#ref-back-37-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-37)[&#8202;](#ref-back-38-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-38)

Lagos is best known as the leading financial centre of West and Central Africa, with headquarters of financial institutions like [Access Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Bank_Group "Access Bank Group"), the [First Bank of Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Bank_of_Nigeria "First Bank of Nigeria") or [Zenith Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenith_Bank "Zenith Bank"). The [Nigerian Exchange Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Exchange_Group "Nigerian Exchange Group"), the main stock exchange group in Nigeria, is also located here. Other major employers include two breweries, three major shipyards and the headquarters of several African [airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airline "Airline"). Lagos is also home to numerous pharmaceutical companies;[&#8202;](#ref-back-39-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-39) the city is also an exhibition city: [Art X Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ART_X_Lagos "ART X Lagos") is West Africa’s largest art fair,[&#8202;](#ref-back-West_Africa's_Capital_Of_Culture-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-West_Africa's_Capital_Of_Culture) whilst [Lagos Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Fashion_Week "Lagos Fashion Week") is Africa’s largest fashion fair.[&#8202;](#ref-back-SystemMagazineInterviewOmoyemiAkerele-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-SystemMagazineInterviewOmoyemiAkerele) It has more than 20 data centres in operation and branches of [Microsoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft "Microsoft"), [Equinix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinix "Equinix") and [Google](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google "Google").[&#8202;](#ref-back-42-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-42) Five out of seven African high tech start-ups with a value above US$ 1 [billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billion "Billion") (" [unicorns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unicorn_(finance) "Unicorn (finance)")") are located in Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-43-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-43) The employer with the most staff is the [Dangote Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangote_refinery "Dangote refinery"), which, once expanded, will be the world’s largest oil refinery.[&#8202;](#ref-back-44-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-44)

Lagos houses four public universities ( [LASU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_University "Lagos State University"), [UNILAG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Lagos "University of Lagos"), [LASUSTECH](https://lasustech.edu.ng/), and [LASUED](https://lasued.edu.ng/web/)) and six private universities as well as scientific facilities like the [Institute for Oceanography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Institute_for_Oceanography_and_Marine_Research "Nigerian Institute for Oceanography and Marine Research") and the [Institute of Medical Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Institute_of_Medical_Research "Nigerian Institute of Medical Research"). Lagos is also a cultural hub. The [National Arts Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre,_Nigeria "National Theatre, Nigeria") in [Surulere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surulere "Surulere"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-45-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-45) the National Gallery of Modern Art (in the same building), the [Centre For Black And African Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_Black_and_African_Arts_and_Civilization "Centre for Black and African Arts and Civilization") on Broad Street, the [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_National_Museum "Nigerian National Museum") and the [John Randle Centre for Yoruba Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Randle_Centre_for_Yoruba_Culture_and_History "John Randle Centre for Yoruba Culture and History")[&#8202;](#ref-back-46-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-46) in Onikan and privately run galleries like [Nike Art Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike_Art_Gallery "Nike Art Gallery") (West Africa's biggest art collection[&#8202;](#ref-back-47-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-47)) display Nigerias culture. The conservatoire [MUSON Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muson_Centre "Muson Centre"), many local orchestras[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-48)[&#8202;](#ref-back-49-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-49) and choirs (like the Lagos City Chorale[&#8202;](#ref-back-50-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-50) or the Cathedral Church of Christ Choir[&#8202;](#ref-back-51-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-51)) as well as writers like [Sefi Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sefi_Atta "Sefi Atta"), [Oyinkan Braithwaite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyinkan_Braithwaite "Oyinkan Braithwaite") and [Ayòbámi Adébáyò](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayobami_Adebayo "Ayobami Adebayo") and artists like [Dotun Popoola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dotun_Popoola "Dotun Popoola"), [Peju Alatise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peju_Alatise "Peju Alatise"), Idowu Sonaya and Collins Abinoro Akporode represent Lagos' creative scene. Furthermore, Lagos is the centre of media outlets and of Nigerian cinema, " [Nollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nollywood "Nollywood")". The lagoon city also is a hot spot for West African music (" [Afrobeats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrobeats "Afrobeats")"); in its recording studios Nigerian stars like [Wizkid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wizkid "Wizkid"), [Tems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tems "Tems"), [Davido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davido "Davido"), and [Asake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asake "Asake") have started their careers. International stars like [Paul McCartney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_McCartney "Paul McCartney"), [James Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brown "James Brown"), [Ed Sheeran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Sheeran "Ed Sheeran") and [Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9 "Beyoncé") have recorded albums there.

## Etymology

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Etymology")\]

_Lagos_ is derived from the Portuguese word for "lakes". The pronunciation [/ˈleɪɡɒs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") ( [_LAY-goss_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")) is typically standard in [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_English "British English") and [Nigerian English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_English "Nigerian English").[&#8202;](#ref-back-wells-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-wells)[&#8202;](#ref-back-hd-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-hd) The native [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language "Yoruba language") name _Èkó_ is also used by [Yoruba people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people "Yoruba people"). Lagos was most likely named after [Lagos, Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos,_Portugal "Lagos, Portugal"), as it was the main centre of Portuguese maritime expeditions down the African coast in the 15th century.[&#8202;](#ref-back-54-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-54)

## History

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: History")\]

Main article: [History of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Lagos "History of Lagos")

Further information: [Lagos Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Colony "Lagos Colony")

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Lagos "Timeline of Lagos").

The [Island of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Island "Lagos Island") ( [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language "Yoruba language"): _Ìsàlẹ̀ Èkó_) emerged as a home to the [Awori people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Awori_people "Awori people"), a subgroup of the [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people "Yoruba people") of [West Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Africa "West Africa") in the 15th century. Before then, the Awori lived in the areas centred around the present-day littoral [Local Government Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lagos_State_local_government_areas_by_population "List of Lagos State local government areas by population") of [Lagos Mainland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Mainland "Lagos Mainland"), [Eti-Osa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eti-Osa "Eti-Osa"), [Amuwo-Odofin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amuwo-Odofin "Amuwo-Odofin"), [Apapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apapa "Apapa") and other parts of Lagos metropolis behind the coast, such as [Agege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agege "Agege"), [Shomolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shomolu "Shomolu"), [Ikeja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikeja "Ikeja"), [Oshodi-Isolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oshodi-Isolo "Oshodi-Isolo") and [Kosofe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosofe "Kosofe"). Lagos Island itself was a farmstead close to the [Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Lagoon "Lagos Lagoon") (Ọ̀sà), where they cultivated the land and lived. "Farmstead" or "hamlet" translates to _Ereko_ in Yoruba,[&#8202;](#ref-back-55-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-55) from which comes the indigenous name of Lagos; _Eko_, which it was named by the natives from its earliest days as a farm before it was eventually permanently settled by the Aworis of nearby [Iddo Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iddo_Island "Iddo Island").[&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-56):43–44

Due to rapid urbanisation, the city expanded to the west of the lagoon to include areas in the present-day [Lagos Mainland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Mainland "Lagos Mainland"), [Ajeromi-Ifelodun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajeromi-Ifelodun "Ajeromi-Ifelodun"), and [Surulere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surulere "Surulere"). This led to the classification of Lagos into two main areas: the [Island](#Island), which was the original city of Lagos, and the [Mainland](#Mainland), which it has since expanded into.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lagos_Case_Study-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-Lagos_Case_Study) This city area was governed directly by the Federal Government through the Lagos City Council until the creation of Lagos State in 1967, which led to the splitting of Lagos city into the present-day seven Local Government Areas (LGAs), and the addition of other towns (which now make up 13 LGAs) from the then [Western Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Region,_Nigeria "Western Region, Nigeria") to form the state.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lagos_State_Information-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-Lagos_State_Information) Present-day Lagos consists of 16 LGAs, including Ikeja, the state capital of Lagos State.[&#8202;](#ref-back-metropolitan_Lagos-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-metropolitan_Lagos)[&#8202;](#ref-back-59-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-59) While this conurbation encompasses 37% of Lagos State's total land area, it accommodates approximately 85% of the state's total population.[&#8202;](#ref-back-metropolitan_Lagos-1)[\[5\]](#refnote-metropolitan_Lagos)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lagos_State_Information-1)[\[56\]](#refnote-Lagos_State_Information)[&#8202;](#ref-back-60-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-60)

Lagos was the previous national capital of Nigeria, however, in February 1976, [the government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Nigeria "Government of Nigeria") made the decision to relocate the capital, and initiated its removal to the inland city of [Abuja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abuja "Abuja") in the centre of the country. After its designation, construction of the new national capital started in earnest, and on December 12 1991, the seat of government officially relocated.[&#8202;](#ref-back-61-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-61)[&#8202;](#ref-back-62-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-62)[&#8202;](#ref-back-63-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-63)

## Administration

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Administration")\]

Lagos was formerly the capital city of [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria"), but it has since been replaced by [Abuja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abuja "Abuja"). Abuja officially became the capital of Nigeria on 12 December 1991, although the decision to move the federal capital had been made fifteen years earlier, in Act No. 6 of 1976. Lagos is also home to the High Court of the [Lagos State Judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_Judiciary "Lagos State Judiciary"), housed in an old colonial building on Lagos Island.[&#8202;](#ref-back-64-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-64)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/1b/Map_of_the_Local_Government_Areas_of_Lagos.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Map_of_the_Local_Government_Areas_of_Lagos.png) A map showing the 16 [LGAs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Government_Areas_in_Nigeria "Local Government Areas in Nigeria") making up the Lagos Metropolitan Area

In terms of administration, Lagos is not a single [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality "Municipality") and therefore has no overall city administration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-65-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-65) The geographical city limits of Metropolitan Lagos comprise 16 of the 20 Local Government Areas of Lagos State. The latter entity provides overall government for the metropolitan region. The former Municipality of Lagos, which covered Lagos Island, Ikoyi, and Victoria Island as well as some mainland territory, was managed by the Lagos City Council (LCC), but it was disbanded in 1976 and divided into several [Local Government Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Government_Areas_in_Nigeria "Local Government Areas in Nigeria") (most notably Lagos Island LGA, [Lagos Mainland LGA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Mainland "Lagos Mainland"), and [Eti-Osa LGA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eti-Osa "Eti-Osa")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-66-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-66)

The mainland beyond the Municipality of Lagos, on the other hand, included several separate towns and settlements such as [Mushin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mushin,_Nigeria "Mushin, Nigeria"), Ikeja, and Agege. In the wake of the 1970s Nigerian oil boom, Lagos experienced a population explosion, untamed economic growth, and unmitigated rural migration. This caused the outlying towns and settlements to develop rapidly, thus forming the present-day "Lagos Metropolitan Area", also known as "Metropolitan Lagos". The history of Lagos is still evidenced in the layout of the LGAs that display the unique identities of the cultures that created them.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\][&#8202;](#ref-back-67-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-67)

By 2006, the metro area around Lagos had extended beyond Lagos State's boundaries and attained a [megacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megacity "Megacity") status. This much larger area is referred to as "Greater Metropolitan Lagos" or "Lagos Megacity Region", which is a continuously built-up land area of an additional 1,535.4 square kilometres (592.8 [square miles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Square_mile "Square mile")), in LGAs situated next to Lagos's eastern and western city limits in Lagos State, and also beyond its northern limits, spilling into some LGAs in adjoining [Ogun State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogun_State "Ogun State"). Ogun State LGAs that have become part of Greater Metropolitan Lagos include [Obafemi Owode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obafemi_Owode "Obafemi Owode"), [Sagamu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sagamu "Sagamu"), [Ifo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ifo,_Ogun_State "Ifo, Ogun State"), [Ado-Odo/Ota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ado-Odo/Ota "Ado-Odo/Ota"), and part of [Ewekoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ewekoro "Ewekoro").[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_sustainable_city-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-The_sustainable_city)

Today, the word _Lagos_ most often refers to the urban area, called "Metropolitan Lagos" in Nigeria, which includes both the islands of the former municipality of Lagos and the mainland suburbs. The Lagos State government is responsible for some of the utilities, including roads and transportation, power, water, health, and education. Metropolitan Lagos extends over 16 of the 20 LGAs of [Lagos State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State "Lagos State") and contains about 85% of the population of Lagos State, including some semi-rural areas.[&#8202;](#ref-back-METRO-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-METRO) Lagos has a considerable number of high-rise buildings that dominate its skyline. Most of the tall buildings are located in the downtown Central Business District.[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-70)[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-71)

## Demography

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Demography")\]

The population of Metropolitan Lagos is disputed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-72-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-72) In the 2006 federal census data, the conurbation had a population of about 9 million people.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Metropolitan_Lagos_population-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-Metropolitan_Lagos_population) However, the figure was disputed by the [Lagos State Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_Government "Lagos State Government"), which later released its own population data, putting the population of [Lagos Metropolitan Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Metropolitan_Area_Transport_Authority "Lagos Metropolitan Area Transport Authority") at approximately 16 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LGA-2)[\[note 1\]](#refnote-LGA) Daily, the Lagos area is growing by some 3,000 people or around 1.1 million annually, so the true population figure of the greater Lagos area in 2022 is roughly 28 million (up from some 23.5 million in 2018).

Lagos may therefore have overtaken [Kinshasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinshasa "Kinshasa") as Africa's most populous city.[&#8202;](#ref-back-metrolagospop-1)[\[4\]](#refnote-metrolagospop)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lagos_State_Information-2)[\[56\]](#refnote-Lagos_State_Information)[&#8202;](#ref-back-74-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-74)[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_sustainable_city-1)[\[66\]](#refnote-The_sustainable_city) The Lagos conurbation is part of an emerging transnational [megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalopolis "Megalopolis") on the coast of West Africa that includes areas in five [sovereign states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_state "Sovereign state"), the [Abidjan–Lagos Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abidjan%E2%80%93Lagos_Corridor "Abidjan–Lagos Corridor").[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[73\]](#refnote-75)[&#8202;](#ref-back-76-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-76)

The 16 LGAs of Metropolitan Lagos

Although the 2006 National Population Census of [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria") credited the metropolitan area with a population figure of 7,937,932, the figure is at variance with some projections by the United Nations and other population agencies and groups worldwide. The population figure of [Lagos State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State "Lagos State") given by the Lagos State Government is 17,553,924. That figure was based on claimed conducted enumeration for social planning by the Lagos State Government's "parallel census" and it believes that since the inhabitants of the metropolitan area of Lagos constitute 88% of the Lagos State population, the population of metropolitan Lagos is about 15.5 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-79-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-79)

A rejoinder to Lagos State Government views[&#8202;](#ref-back-80-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-80) concluded that Lagos State concealed the fact that the UN agencies' population projection for Lagos Urban Agglomeration had been revised downwards substantially as early as 2003. It failed to interpret the two most important and fairly representative and reliable secondary data sets already in the public domain, the National Identity Card Scheme and the 2003 Voters Registration figures from INEC. The figures for 2007 voter registration by INEC were an act sequel to the release of the provisional census results and comprehensively corroborated, vindicated, and validated the population figures.
According to the official results of the 2006 census, there were 8,048,430 inhabitants in Metropolitan Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Metropolitan_Lagos_population-2)[\[71\]](#refnote-Metropolitan_Lagos_population) This figure was lower than anticipated and has created controversy in Nigeria. Lagos Island, the central Local Government Area and historic center of Metropolitan Lagos, had a population of 212,700 at the 2006 Census.[&#8202;](#ref-back-81-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-81)

Authorities of Lagos State have disputed the results of the 2006 census, accusing the Nigerian National Population Commission of undercounting the population of the state. This accusation is denied by the National Population Commission.[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-82)[&#8202;](#ref-back-83-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-83) A study found that research carried out by Africapolis (the African subsidiary of e-Geopolis backed by the [Agence française de développement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Development_Agency "French Development Agency")), in addition to the cross-referencing of official figures with more scientific independent research, concluded that the 2006 census figures for Lagos State of about 9 million were valid and that the state's own assessments are inflated.[&#8202;](#ref-back-84-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-84)

Lagos is indigenous to the [Yoruba people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people "Yoruba people"), and the [Yoruba language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language "Yoruba language") is widely spoken. It is, by most estimates, one of the fastest-growing cities in the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-africareport-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-africareport) Lagos is experiencing a population increase of about 275,000 persons per annum due to the influx of people from other regions for economic purposes. In 1999, the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations "United Nations") predicted that the city's metropolitan area, which had only about 290,000 inhabitants in 1950, would exceed 20 million by 2010 and thus become one of the ten most populous cities in the world.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\][&#8202;](#ref-back-86-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-86)

## Geography

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Geography")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Lagos_Map.PNG/250px-Lagos_Map.PNG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos_Map.PNG) Map of Lagos's initial city boundaries, showing its contemporary districts. This definition is rarely used nowadays; the expanded metropolitan area is now a more accepted definition of Lagos.[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Lagos%2C_Nigeria.jpg/250px-Lagos%2C_Nigeria.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos,_Nigeria.jpg) Satellite image of Lagos

### Cityscape

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Cityscape")\]

Lagos is loosely classified into two main geographical areas—"Lagos Island" and the "Mainland".[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-88)[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[87\]](#refnote-89)

The historic city centre is situated in the western half of Lagos Island, within the Ring Road (E1), with Marina Road, Broad Street, Campbell Street, Igbosere Road and Bamgbose Street/Nnamdi Azikiwe Street running parallel to the coastline towards the Commodore Canal, and Balogun Street, Martin Street, Custom Street, Kakawa Street, Odunlami Street, Campos Street, Joseph Harden Street and Brook Street running perpendicular to it. As in the historic centres of other cities, this area is characterised by social hotspots, chaotic traffic conditions, unaffordable property prices and historic buildings, the proper maintenance of which the city can only afford to a limited extent.

Outside this area, there is a marked disparity in prosperity within the city,[&#8202;](#ref-back-VIvsIkoyi-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-VIvsIkoyi) with the south-eastern part – comprising neighbourhoods such as [Ikoyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikoyi "Ikoyi"), [Victoria Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Island "Victoria Island") and [Lekki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki "Lekki"), home to bank headquarters and fintech start-ups, the Free (Trade) Zone, the deep-sea port, the Dangote Refinery, hotels and tourist attractions – being described as affluent.[&#8202;](#ref-back-VIvsIkoyi-1)[\[88\]](#refnote-VIvsIkoyi) By contrast, the western and northern outskirts of the city, with neighbourhoods such as Surelere, Oshodi and Agege, have been able to keep up only to a limited extent with the city’s rapid development since 2010.[&#8202;](#ref-back-91-0)[\[89\]](#refnote-91)

#### Lagos Island

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Lagos Island")\]

Main article: [Lagos Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Island "Lagos Island")

Lagos Island is surrounded by the lagoon (from which it takes its name), the Commodore Channel (which separates the island from the mainland and Apapa) and Five Cowrie Creek (which lies between it and Victoria Island respectively the Lekki Peninsula). During the colonial era, the eastern half of Lagos Island – Ikoyi – was home mainly to light-skinned residents (including a secondary school and a hospital for ‘whites’). To enforce racial segregation, an artificial canal – the McGregor Canal – was dug between the two halves of the island around 1900. This effectively made Lagos Island and Ikoyi two separate islands.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:21-0)[\[90\]](#refnote-:21) In the 1970s, the canal was filled in to allow for the construction of the Ring Road (E1) around the old town centre. As a result, Ikoyi is, geographically speaking, once again part of Lagos Island – yet both halves of the island continue to exhibit completely different social and architectural structures to this day and can therefore still be regarded as distinct neighbourhoods.

Three major bridges join this Island to the Mainland. They are the [Carter Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carter_Bridge "Carter Bridge"), which starts from Iddo; the [Eko Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eko_Bridge "Eko Bridge") (formerly called the Second Mainland Bridge); and the [Third Mainland Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Mainland_Bridge "Third Mainland Bridge"), which passes through densely populated mainland suburbs to the Lagos Lagoon.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Lagos-Island-skyline-Heliconia-Park-aug-2026-IMG_2125.jpg/250px-Lagos-Island-skyline-Heliconia-Park-aug-2026-IMG_2125.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos-Island-skyline-Heliconia-Park-aug-2026-IMG_2125.jpg) Lagos Island, view from Eko Atlantic (2026)

The western part of Lagos Island, together with [Calabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calabar "Calabar"), was one of the two starting points for British colonisation. From this strategically situated island, the British were able to control the entire wider region with little effort. Locals and slaves returning from [Sierra Leone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Leone "Sierra Leone"), [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil "Brazil") and [Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba "Cuba") were able to conduct business under the protection of the [Royal Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Navy "Royal Navy"), and turned the marshy island into what it remains today: a pre-eminent financial centre.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:21-1)[\[90\]](#refnote-:21)

Lagos Island contains a [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_business_district "Central business district").[&#8202;](#ref-back-93-0)[\[91\]](#refnote-93) This district is characterized by high-rise buildings. The Island also contains many of the city's largest wholesale marketplaces (such as the popular [Idumota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idumota_Market "Idumota Market") and [Balogun Markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balogun_Market "Balogun Market")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-94-0)[\[92\]](#refnote-94) It also has the National Museum of Nigeria, the Central Mosque, the [Glover Memorial Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glover_Memorial_Hall "Glover Memorial Hall"), [Christ's Church Cathedral (CMS)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Church_of_Christ,_Lagos "Cathedral Church of Christ, Lagos"), and the [Oba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oba_of_Lagos "Oba of Lagos")'s [Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iga_Idunganran "Iga Idunganran") ( [Iga Idunganran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iga_Idunganran "Iga Idunganran")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-95-0)[\[93\]](#refnote-95) Another major part of Lagos Island is Marina. It borders the Idumota and Balogun markets and houses major Banking institutions. Though formerly in a derelict condition, Lagos Island's [Tinubu Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tinubu_Square "Tinubu Square") is a site of historical importance; it was here that the Amalgamation Ceremony that unified the North and South [protectorates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectorate "Protectorate") to form Nigeria took place in 1914.[&#8202;](#ref-back-96-0)[\[94\]](#refnote-96)

##### Ikoyi

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Ikoyi")\]

Main article: [Ikoyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikoyi "Ikoyi")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Ikoyi%2C_Lagos%2C_Nigeria.jpg/250px-Ikoyi%2C_Lagos%2C_Nigeria.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ikoyi,_Lagos,_Nigeria.jpg) Aerial view of Ikoyi (Victoria Island in the background)

[Ikoyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikoyi "Ikoyi") is situated on the eastern half of Lagos Island.[&#8202;](#ref-back-97-0)[\[95\]](#refnote-97)[&#8202;](#ref-back-98-0)[\[96\]](#refnote-98) Ikoyi is connected to Victoria Island by Falomo bridge, which carries the main road over Five Cowrie creek.[&#8202;](#ref-back-99-0)[\[97\]](#refnote-99) The Ikoyi link bridge links Ikoyi and Lekki Phase 1.[&#8202;](#ref-back-100-0)[\[98\]](#refnote-100)

Since colonial times, Ikoyi has been a neighbourhood for the more affluent, although since independence in 1960 it has no longer been exclusively for light-skinned people. Wide, well-maintained avenues with bicycle lanes and ostentatious buildings belonging to the nouveau riche characterise this neighbourhood[&#8202;](#ref-back-101-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-101) – named after the British governors who administered Nigeria from Lagos (such as Bourdillon). Alfred Rewane Road is home to the offices of international companies such as [Microsoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft "Microsoft"), [Google](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google "Google"), [Dangote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangote_Group "Dangote Group"), [Lufthansa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufthansa "Lufthansa"), [Samsung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samsung "Samsung"), [BAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_American_Tobacco "British American Tobacco") and others. The Ikoyi Club 1938, which was also originally an exclusively ‘white’ establishment,[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[100\]](#refnote-102) remains exclusive and retains its status as one of the largest and prestigious golf courses in Africa[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-103) despite the astronomical property prices in the surrounding area. The [Dodan Barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodan_Barracks "Dodan Barracks") (originally built in Ikoyi by the colonial rulers to protect them against uprisings from other parts of the city) served as Nigeria’s centre of power during the military dictatorships until the 1990s, when Abuja became the new capital.[&#8202;](#ref-back-104-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-104) Military ruler [Murtala Muhammed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murtala_Muhammed "Murtala Muhammed") was assassinated by coup plotters in 1976 near the barracks on Ikoyi Road – a memorial commemorates him. [Radio Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Radio_Corporation_of_Nigeria "Federal Radio Corporation of Nigeria") has been broadcasting from Ikoyi since colonial times and was for a long time the country’s only mass medium.[&#8202;](#ref-back-105-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-105) The Federal High Court of Nigeria also resides in Ikoyi.[&#8202;](#ref-back-106-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-106)

##### Victoria Island

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Victoria Island")\]

Main article: [Victoria Island (Nigeria)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Island_(Nigeria) "Victoria Island (Nigeria)")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Eko_Atlantic_%28Lagos%29_Skyline.jpg/250px-Eko_Atlantic_%28Lagos%29_Skyline.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eko_Atlantic_(Lagos)_Skyline.jpg) [Eko Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eko_Atlantic "Eko Atlantic"), a project at the mouth of [Lagos Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Lagoon "Lagos Lagoon") under construction, extending and further developing Victoria Island (as seen from [Tarkwa Bay Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarkwa_Bay_Beach "Tarkwa Bay Beach"))

Victoria Island with its annex is situated to the south of Lagos Island and known with a zip code of 101241[&#8202;](#ref-back-107-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-107) as assigned by NIPOST.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Conference-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-Conference) It has expensive real estate properties and for that reason, many new luxury condos and apartments.
Along with Ikoyi, Victoria Island occupies a major area in Lagos that boasts several shopping districts. On its seashore along the Atlantic front, there is an environmentally reconstructed [Bar Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Beach,_Lagos "Bar Beach, Lagos").[&#8202;](#ref-back-109-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-109)

##### Ajah/Lekki

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Ajah/Lekki")\]

The Lekki Peninsula shares some prestige with its Ikoyi and Victoria Island neighbours. Development has stretched the piece of land further such that the Ibeju axis, though closer to Epe (which is on the outskirts of Lagos) is almost always described as part of Lekki. The expanse of land starts from the Lekki toll gate, which was the focal stage of the famous #EndSars protest in October 2020, and ends in Ibeju-Lekki and boasts of communities slowly inching their way to suburb status such as [Ajah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajah,_Lagos "Ajah, Lagos"), Awoyaya, Sangotedo, Abijo, and Eputu. There is quite a bit of place to see – the [Lekki Conservation Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Conservation_Centre "Lekki Conservation Centre"); The Novare Mall; The [Lekki Free Trade Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Free_Trade_Zone "Lekki Free Trade Zone") – Dangote, Africa's richest man is building his refinery in this FTZ; [Lagos Business School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Business_School "Lagos Business School"); [Eleko Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleko_Beach "Eleko Beach"); [Elegushi Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegushi_Beach "Elegushi Beach"); [La Campagne Tropicana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Campagne_Tropicana "La Campagne Tropicana") – a beach/tourist getaway, [Pan-Atlantic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Atlantic_University "Pan-Atlantic University"). The area has a Catholic monastery.[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-110)

##### Iddo

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Iddo")\]

Across the main channel of the lagoon from Lagos Island, there is a smaller settlement called [Iddo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iddo_Island "Iddo Island"). Iddo is also [a railroad terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Terminus_railway_station "Lagos Terminus railway station") and it is now situated in the Lagos Mainland Local Government Area after it was connected to the Mainland like a peninsula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-111)

#### Mainland

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Mainland")\]

A huge population of Lagosians also live on the Lagos Mainland, and most industries are located there. The Mainland is known for its music and nightlife, which used to be located in areas around [Yaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaba,_Lagos "Yaba, Lagos"), Ikeja, and [Surulere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surulere "Surulere"). However, in recent years more nightclubs have sprung up on the Island, making the Island (particularly Victoria Island, Ikate, and Lekki Phase 1) the main nightlife attraction. Mainland LGAs include Surulere, [Apapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apapa "Apapa"), and [Lagos Mainland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Mainland "Lagos Mainland"). Metropolitan Lagos suburban LGAs include: [Agege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agege "Agege"), [Amuwo Odofin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amuwo_Odofin "Amuwo Odofin"), [Mushin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mushin,_Lagos "Mushin, Lagos"), [Oshodi-Isolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oshodi-Isolo "Oshodi-Isolo") and Ikeja (site of [Murtala Muhammed International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murtala_Muhammed_International_Airport "Murtala Muhammed International Airport") and the capital of Lagos State).[&#8202;](#ref-back-112-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-112)

Major areas on the Mainland include [Ebute Metta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebute_Metta "Ebute Metta"), [Yaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaba,_Lagos "Yaba, Lagos") and [Ejigbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ejigbo,_Lagos "Ejigbo, Lagos"). Some rivers, like [Badagry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badagry "Badagry") Creek, flow parallel to the coast for some distance before exiting through the sand bars to the sea.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\][&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-113)[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-114)

## Climate

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Climate")\]

Lagos experiences a [tropical savanna climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate "Tropical savanna climate") ( _Aw_) according to the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification"), as there are three months under 60 millimetres or 2.4 inches of rain, and annual rainfall is not nearly high enough for [tropical monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_monsoon_climate "Tropical monsoon climate") classification. The wet season starts in May and ends in October, while the dry season starts in November and ends in April. There is also a short dry season from July to August. The wettest month is June with precipitation total 316 millimetres or 12.44 inches, while the driest month is January with precipitation total 13 millimetres or 0.51 inches.[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-115)[&#8202;](#ref-back-116-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-116)

Located near the equator, Lagos has only a slight seasonal temperature variation, with mean high temperatures ranging from 28 to 32°C (82 to 90°F). Lagos has the highest temperatures in March with a daily range from 26 to 32°C (79 to 90°F), and least hot temperatures in August ranging from 24 to 28°C (75 to 82°F), not unlike the seasons of coastal locations in the Southern Hemisphere. Summer is the coolest season, with June and September are the coldest and rainiest months, while spring (March - May) is the hottest season.[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-117)[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-118)
