### Climate change

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Climate change")\]

A 2019 paper published in [PLOS One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLOS_One "PLOS One") estimated that under [Representative Concentration Pathway 4.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_Concentration_Pathway#4.5 "Representative Concentration Pathway"), a "moderate" scenario of [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") where global warming reaches ~2.5–3°C (4.5–5.4°F) by 2100, the climate of Lagos in the year 2050 would most closely resemble the current climate of [Panama City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_City "Panama City"). The annual temperature would increase by 1.6°C (2.9°F) and the temperature of the warmest month by 1.5°C (2.7°F), while the temperature of the coldest month would be 2.9°C (5.2°F) higher.[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-123)[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-124) According to [Climate Action Tracker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_Action_Tracker "Climate Action Tracker"), the current warming trajectory appears consistent with 2.7°C (4.9°F), which closely matches RCP 4.5.[&#8202;](#ref-back-125-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-125)

Moreover, according to the 2022 [IPCC Sixth Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPCC_Sixth_Assessment_Report "IPCC Sixth Assessment Report"), Lagos is one of 12 major African cities ( [Abidjan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abidjan "Abidjan"), [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria "Alexandria"), [Algiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers "Algiers"), [Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town "Cape Town"), [Casablanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casablanca "Casablanca"), [Dakar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dakar "Dakar"), [Dar es Salaam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dar_es_Salaam "Dar es Salaam"), [Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durban "Durban"), Lagos, [Lomé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lom%C3%A9 "Lomé"), [Luanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luanda "Luanda") and [Maputo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maputo "Maputo")) that would be the most severely affected by [sea level rise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level_rise "Sea level rise"). It estimates that they would collectively sustain cumulative damage of US$65 billion under RCP 4.5 and US$86.5 billion in the high-emission scenario RCP 8.5 by the year 2050. Additionally, RCP 8.5 combined with the hypothetical impact from [marine ice sheet instability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_ice_sheet_instability "Marine ice sheet instability") at high levels of warming would involve up to US$137.5 billion in damage, while the additional accounting for the "low-probability, high-damage events" may increase aggregate risks to US$187 billion for the "moderate" RCP4.5, US$206 billion for RCP8.5 and US$397 billion under the high-end ice sheet instability scenario.[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-126) Since sea level rise would continue for about 10,000 years under every scenario of climate change, future costs of sea level rise would only increase, especially without adaptation measures.[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-127) Sea level rise is being exacerbated by [subsidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidence "Subsidence"), which is occurring at up to 87 millimetres (3.4in) per year.[&#8202;](#ref-back-128-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-128)

## Economy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Economy")\]

The city of Lagos is a major economic focal point in Nigeria, generating around 30-35% of the country's GDP. Most commercial and financial businesses are carried out in the [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_business_districts "Central business districts") situated on the island. This is also where most of the country's commercial banks, financial institutions, and major corporations are headquartered. Lagos is also the major information communications and telecommunications (ICT) hub of West Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-129)[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-130) Lagos is developing a [24-hour economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24/7_service "24/7 service").[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-131)[&#8202;](#ref-back-nightlife-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-nightlife) Yves Bellinghausen summed up the city’s economy as follows: 'Lagos is Africa's [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles "Hollywood, Los Angeles"), [Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan "Manhattan"), and [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley "Silicon Valley") all rolled into one.'[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-133)

The globalisation of Lagos's economy is rated "beta minus" by the GaWC.[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-134) This is equivalent to [Manchester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester "Manchester") or [Edinburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edinburgh "Edinburgh") in the [UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom "United Kingdom"). Lagos is thus the most "globalised" city in West and Central Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-135-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-135) 5 out of 7 African tech " [unicorns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unicorn_(finance) "Unicorn (finance)")" operate out of Lagos (see below).[&#8202;](#ref-back-:1-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-:1) Lagos is home to more tech centres than any other city in Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-:0)

### Financial institutions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Financial institutions")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/An_array_of_iconic_buildings_in_Lagos_Nigeria_starting_with_United_Bank_of_Africa.jpg/250px-An_array_of_iconic_buildings_in_Lagos_Nigeria_starting_with_United_Bank_of_Africa.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:An_array_of_iconic_buildings_in_Lagos_Nigeria_starting_with_United_Bank_of_Africa.jpg) Financial district of Lagos

Lagos is a major financial and banking centre. The four largest banks in West and Central Africa are headquartered in Lagos,[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-:5) and another nine banks in Lagos are among the 20 largest banks in the region. [Zenith Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenith_Bank "Zenith Bank"), [Access Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Bank_plc "Access Bank plc"), [Guaranty Trust Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaranty_Trust_Holding_Company_PLC "Guaranty Trust Holding Company PLC") and [First Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Bank_of_Nigeria "First Bank of Nigeria") have capital of more than US$2 billion each.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-1)[\[136\]](#refnote-:5) Banking headquarters are located on Victoria Island and Lagos Island.

The national stock exchange, the [Nigerian Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Exchange_Group "Nigerian Exchange Group"), is also located here (in the background on the right in the adjacent picture). Furthermore, the NASD OTC Securities Exchange (on the left, in the light-coloured building) and the FMDQ Group are based here on [Victoria Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Island,_Lagos "Victoria Island, Lagos").

The insurance industry in Nigeria is comparatively and modestly developed, with an industry turnover of around US$1 billion per year.[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-139) As with the banks, the headquarters of the insurance companies are predominantly located in Lagos.

### Ports, shipyards

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Ports, shipyards")\]

Main articles: [Apapa Port Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apapa_Port_Complex "Apapa Port Complex"), [Tin Can Island Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tin_Can_Island_Port "Tin Can Island Port"), and [Lekki Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Port "Lekki Port")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Lagos-Nigeria-Lekki-Deep-Sea-Port-aug-2026-IMG_6279.jpg/250px-Lagos-Nigeria-Lekki-Deep-Sea-Port-aug-2026-IMG_6279.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos-Nigeria-Lekki-Deep-Sea-Port-aug-2026-IMG_6279.jpg) Entrance of the Lekki Deep Sea Port

The Port of Lagos, formally known as the Lagos-Elbert Mathews Memorial Port, is Nigeria's leading [port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port "Port") and one of the largest and busiest Ports in Africa. Due to the large urban population, Lagos is categorized as a medium-port megacity using the Southampton System for port-city classification.[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-140) It is administered by the [Nigerian Ports Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Ports_Authority "Nigerian Ports Authority").

The _Port of Lagos / Apapa_ is the oldest and largest port in the country, both in terms of land area and cargo volume handled. More than half of Nigeria's maritime trade is handled here, and the port also acts as a transhipment point for landlocked countries such as Chad and Niger. Around 1,000 ships with 5,700,000 tonnes of cargo call at the Lagos port complex annually.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-:4) _Tin Can Island Port_ is located west of Apapa near the Lagos Port Complex. It was established in 1975. In early 2023, the [_deep sea port of Lekki_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Port "Lekki Port") was commissioned 50km east of Lagos. This thus does not belong to the urban area of Lagos but to the [state of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State "Lagos State"). In 2025, a decision was taken to modernise the ports of Apapa and Tin Can Island at a cost of one billion US dollars.[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-142)[&#8202;](#ref-back-143-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-143) The fourth (and easternmost) port in Lagos worth mentioning is the port of the [Dangote Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangote_refinery "Dangote refinery"), which supplies the refinery with crude oil from the Niger Delta or, for example, from [Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola "Angola"), and ships out products such as petrol and [kerosene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerosene "Kerosene").

Three of Nigeria’s four largest shipyards are located in Lagos: [Nigerdock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerdock "Nigerdock") (Snake Island), Naval Dockyard Ltd. (Victoria Island) and [Samsung Heavy Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samsung_Heavy_Industries "Samsung Heavy Industries") Mega Construction Yard (Tarkwa Bay). Nigerdock is the largest shipyard in West Africa. It is situated in the Snake Island Integrated Free Zone and boasts the country’s largest dry dock (25,000 DWT).[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-144) It is a major hub for shipbuilding, ship repair and oil and gas production. Naval Dockyard Limited is owned by the [Nigerian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Navy "Nigerian Navy") but serves customers from the merchant shipping and oil and gas sectors. It is one of the most efficient and best-equipped technical facilities in West Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-145) Samsung Heavy Industries Mega Construction Yard is a huge integrated shipyard built primarily for the assembly of extremely large floating production, storage and offloading units (FPSOs) and for heavy shipbuilding.

### Entertainment industry and media

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Entertainment industry and media")\]

#### Nollywood

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Nollywood")\]

Main article: [Cinema of Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Nigeria "Cinema of Nigeria")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Clapper_Lady.jpg/250px-Clapper_Lady.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Clapper_Lady.jpg) Filming set in Lagos

Lagos is the centre of the West African film, music, and TV industries. The film industry in the Surulere locality ranks second or third in the world, ahead of or behind Hollywood, depending on the survey.[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-146) PricewaterhouseCoopers Int. forecasts that the Nigerian entertainment industry will grow 85% to $15 billion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-147)
Since the success of the Nigerian thriller "The Figurine", Nigerian film has increasingly turned to high-quality productions that are also commercially successful. This, in turn, has led to consistently new box office revenue records in Nigeria (2009's "The Figurine", 2013's "Half of a Yellow Sun", 2016's "The Wedding Party", 2023's "Battle on Buka Street").[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-148)

#### Newspapers

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Newspapers")\]

In Nigeria, newspapers are available in digital format and are predominantly produced in Lagos. The most widely read newspaper in Lagos, by its own account, is [Punch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Punch "The Punch"). The [Vanguard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguard_(Nigeria) "Vanguard (Nigeria)") newspaper is one of the few dailies that is not only available online but also in print. Other publications include [The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian_(Nigeria) "The Guardian (Nigeria)"), [The Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Nation_(Nigeria) "The Nation (Nigeria)"), [The Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sun_(Nigeria) "The Sun (Nigeria)") and the [Nigerian Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Tribune "Nigerian Tribune"). The latter was founded in colonial times, in 1949.

#### Television

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Television")\]

The most watched television station in Lagos (and in Nigeria) is the 24-hour news channel [Channels TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channels_TV "Channels TV"), based in Lagos. Some of its presenters use an overly correct standard of British English that compatriots like to mock. The same can be said of [Arise TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arise_News "Arise News") and the state broadcaster [NTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Television_Authority "Nigerian Television Authority"). The private [African Independent Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa_Independent_Television "Africa Independent Television") focuses on entertainment and infotainment. Programmes in [pidgin English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pidgin_English "Pidgin English") or in [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_language "Yoruba language") have moved to digital streaming services and offer [action films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_film "Action film"), [comedies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comedy "Comedy") and [heartbreak productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_film "Romance film").

### "Africa's Silicon Valley"

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: \"Africa's Silicon Valley\"")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Kamdi_Uko_-_Andela_Lagos.jpg/250px-Kamdi_Uko_-_Andela_Lagos.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kamdi_Uko_-_Andela_Lagos.jpg) IT trainer and recruiter Andela, Lagos

Seven tech start-ups in Africa are said to have " [unicorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unicorn_(finance) "Unicorn (finance)")" status (worth over 1 billion euros). Five of them are based in Lagos:[&#8202;](#ref-back-:1-1)[\[134\]](#refnote-:1) _[Flutterwave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flutterwave "Flutterwave")_ is in the virtual bank card business. _Opay_ and _[Interswitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interswitch "Interswitch")_ are platforms for online bookings. _[Andela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andela "Andela")_ trains software engineers and places them in the Nigerian labour market. _[Jumia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumia "Jumia")_ is an online retail company that offers a wide range of products such as electronic devices and fashion.

Lagos is home to more tech hubs than any other city in Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-1)[\[135\]](#refnote-:0) With more than 90 million internet users, Lagos is attracting investors who want to capitalise on this expanding technology hotspot.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-2)[\[135\]](#refnote-:0) Startupgrind.com refers to Lagos as "Africa's Silicon Valley".[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-149) [Bloomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_News "Bloomberg News") highlights "Nigeria's Chaotic Rise as the Tech Heart of Africa" and means _Lagos_, specifically the [Yaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaba,_Lagos "Yaba, Lagos") district.[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-150)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Falomo_view_from_the_bridgeto_MTN_PLaza.jpg/250px-Falomo_view_from_the_bridgeto_MTN_PLaza.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Falomo_view_from_the_bridgeto_MTN_PLaza.jpg) MTN in Lagos

Lagos is the only African city to have both a Google and a Microsoft office. [_MTN_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Group "MTN Group") maintains the first and still predominant 4G network in Nigeria. [Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airtel_Africa "Airtel Africa") is another 4G provider. 9Mobile and Dataflex are Internet providers. [Paystack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paystack "Paystack") is used by Nigerians who regularly receive payments from abroad. [ULesson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ULesson "ULesson") maintains a platform on which secondary school learning content is presented. Hotels.ng allows hotel bookings to be made throughout Africa.[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-151)[&#8202;](#ref-back-152-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-152)

### Oil refinery

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Oil refinery")\]

Main article: [Dangote Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangote_Refinery "Dangote Refinery")

For decades, there was no oil processing industry in Nigeria, apart from illegal refineries in the Niger Delta (which are very polluting due to the lack of [cracking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cracking_(chemistry) "Cracking (chemistry)")). Nigeria therefore had to have the end products of domestic crude oil such as [fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuel "Fuel"), [bitumen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitumen "Bitumen"), [paraffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraffin_(fuel) "Paraffin (fuel)"), [motor oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_oil "Motor oil"), [polypropylene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polypropylene "Polypropylene") etc. produced in US or European refineries, with transport costs over thousands of nautical miles and margins for middlemen. The oil refinery in Lekki went into operation in December 2023[&#8202;](#ref-back-153-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-153) and presently produces 650,000 barrels of oil per day, with plans to expand capacity to 14 million barrels per day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-154-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-154)

In October 2025, Dangote announced plans to more than double the daily capacity of the Lekki refinery to 1.4 million barrels at a cost of USD 350 million. This would make Lekki the largest refinery in the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-155-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-155) During the same period, the neighbouring states of [Ogun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogun_State "Ogun State") and [Ondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ondo_State "Ondo State") announced their own plans to build oil refineries: Ogun on Tongeji Island near [Ipokia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipokia "Ipokia") (close to [Badagry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badagry "Badagry")) and Ondo in the ‘Sunshine Free Trade Zone Igotiri’ in [Ilaje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilaje "Ilaje"), 50km east of the Lekki refinery.[&#8202;](#ref-back-156-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-156)[&#8202;](#ref-back-157-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-157)

### Fertiliser plant

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Fertiliser plant")\]

Since 2022, a new fertiliser production plant has been producing 3 million tonnes of fertiliser a year (roughly equivalent to Germany's fertiliser consumption).[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-:6) With no more Russian fertiliser coming onto the world market in 2022 due to this country's [invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Russian invasion of Ukraine"), Nigeria is stepping into a gap in the market. "The fertiliser market is a seller's market," company owner [Dangote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliko_Dangote "Aliko Dangote") raves. "People are begging for us to sell and we are choosy about who we sell to."[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-1)[\[156\]](#refnote-:6)[&#8202;](#ref-back-159-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-159)

### Pharmaceutical industry

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Pharmaceutical industry")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Pharmacy_ikeja_lagos.jpg/250px-Pharmacy_ikeja_lagos.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Pharmacy_ikeja_lagos.jpg) Pharmacy in [Ikeja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikeja "Ikeja")

Nigeria hosts about 60 percent of the pharmaceutical production capacity in Africa (status 2022).[&#8202;](#ref-back-160-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-160) The larger pharmaceutical companies in Nigeria are located in the North of Lagos.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

### Automotive industry

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Automotive industry")\]

Main article: [Automotive industry in Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Nigeria "Automotive industry in Nigeria")

[Nord Automobile Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nord_Automobile_Limited?action=edit&redlink=1 "Nord Automobile Limited (page does not exist)") has two assembly plants in Lagos: in Sangotedo and in Epe. The company manufactures its own plastic parts and plans to take on steel pressing in the future. The company offers eight different models.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-161) However, company founder and CEO Oluwatobi Ajayi is struggling with insufficient demand and the increase in the price of imported components due to the devaluation of the local currency, the naira, in 2023. He is looking for solutions with a German partner.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-162)

### Lekki Free Trade Zone

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Lekki Free Trade Zone")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Lekki_free_trade_zone.jpg/250px-Lekki_free_trade_zone.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lekki_free_trade_zone.jpg) Entrance of the Lekki Free Trade Zone

The [Lekki Free Trade Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki_Free_Trade_Zone "Lekki Free Trade Zone") is a free trade zone in the eastern part of Lekki, covering a total area of about 155 square kilometres. It has an area of 30 square kilometres and is to be developed into a multifunctional district: integrating industry, trade and commerce, real estate development, warehousing and logistics, tourism, and entertainment.

### Trade fairs and exhibitions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Trade fairs and exhibitions")\]

Lagos has two major congress halls, the Eko Convention Center and the Landmark Centre.[&#8202;](#ref-back-163-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-163) The [Eko Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eko_Convention_Centre "Eko Convention Centre") has 5,151 m2 and 13 meeting rooms. It hosts for example the Lagos Fashion Fair.[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-164) The Landmark Centre has 1,004 m2 and 8 meeting rooms.[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-165) The Landmark Centre hosts annual meetings like "Medic/Medlab West Africa", "Beauty West Africa" or "agro-food".[&#8202;](#ref-back-166-0)[\[164\]](#refnote-166)

The _Lagos Leather Fair_ is the largest leather fair in West Africa. Nigeria is the sixth largest leather exporter in the world, with brands such as Prada, Gucci and Louis Vuitton sourcing their goods here. Fashion journalist Waridi Schrobsdorff even puts 'Milan, Paris, Lagos' on the same level on news channel [N-tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N-tv "N-tv").[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-167)

_[ART X Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ART_X_Lagos "ART X Lagos")_ is an international art fair held annually in Lagos that showcases modern and contemporary art from Africa and the African diaspora.[&#8202;](#ref-back-168-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-168) It is the leading international art fair in [West Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Africa "West Africa").[&#8202;](#ref-back-West_Africa's_Capital_Of_Culture-1)[\[38\]](#refnote-West_Africa's_Capital_Of_Culture)

_[Lagos Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Fashion_Week "Lagos Fashion Week")_ is Africa's largest fashion event drawing considerable media attention.[&#8202;](#ref-back-SystemMagazineInterviewOmoyemiAkerele-1)[\[39\]](#refnote-SystemMagazineInterviewOmoyemiAkerele)

### Food processing and distribution

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: Food processing and distribution")\]

Nigeria's largest _brewery_, fancily named Nigerian Breweries and a Heineken subsidiary, is located in the Lagos-Surulere district. The Guinness brewery produces its famous strong beer in the Ikeja district. Apparently, the average Nigerian drinks larger quantities of this beverage than the average Irishman.[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-169) Both breweries also produce non-alcoholic (Guinness also halal) malt beer, which is part of the "Lagos' way of life".[&#8202;](#ref-back-170-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-170)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Ketu-Ereyun_logistics_park.png/250px-Ketu-Ereyun_logistics_park.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ketu-Ereyun_logistics_park.png) Logistics hub, close to Epe

In Ketu-Ereyun, between [Epe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epe,_Lagos_State "Epe, Lagos State") and [Ikorodu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikorodu "Ikorodu"), Lagos State builds a _" [Food Logistics Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_Logistics_Park_Lagos "Food Logistics Park Lagos")"_ – the biggest logistics hub for food in Sub-Saharan Africa. The site is 1.2 million square meters big and the construction is expected to be finished in 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-171-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-171)[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-172)

Until now, Nigeria paradoxically exported unhusked rice but had to import husked rice, the country's staple food. – The [_hulling mill in Imota_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imota_rice_mill "Imota rice mill"), just outside Lagos, processes the rice domestically. When fully operational, the plant, the largest south of the Sahara, is expected to employ 250,000 people and produce 2.5 million 50-kg bags of rice annually.[&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[171\]](#refnote-173)

The _Apapa sugar refinery_,[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-:8) part of the Dangote Group, increased its turnover to 288.3 billion naira (€590 million) in the third quarter of 2022 – a 47% increase from the third quarter of 2021. The sugar refinery has a capacity of 1.44 million metric tonnes per annum and supplies end users as well as bulk buyers such as [Nestlé Nigeria Plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestle_Nigeria "Nestle Nigeria"), [Cadbury Nigeria Plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadbury_Nigeria_Plc "Cadbury Nigeria Plc"), [Seven-Up Bottling Company Plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven-Up_Bottling_Company "Seven-Up Bottling Company") and the [Nigerian Bottling Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Bottling_Company "Nigerian Bottling Company").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-1)[\[172\]](#refnote-:8)

Lagos State Governor [Babajide Sanwo-Olu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babajide_Sanwo-Olu "Babajide Sanwo-Olu") opened the new Ikosi International _Fruits Market in the Ketu_ district on 25 May 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-:2) The new fruit market comprises 1,004 shop units.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-:3) It has its own water and electricity supply, canteen and parking facilities. The facility is monitored by security personnel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-1)[\[174\]](#refnote-:3) On 18 December 2023, Sanwo-Olu opened a similar "Fresh Food Hub" in Idi-Oro, [Mushin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mushin,_Lagos "Mushin, Lagos").[&#8202;](#ref-back-177-0)[\[175\]](#refnote-177)[&#8202;](#ref-back-178-0)[\[176\]](#refnote-178) Both hubs are aiming to increase the speed of food supply and reduce the percentage of food going to waste before it reaches the customer.

### Timber, sawmill

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Timber, sawmill")\]

The Lagos sawmill moved from its old but bursting-at-the-seams location in Oko Baba to [Timberville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timberville_(Lagos) "Timberville (Lagos)"), just outside Lagos, in 2022, where modern facilities are available.[&#8202;](#ref-back-179-0)[\[177\]](#refnote-179)[&#8202;](#ref-back-180-0)[\[178\]](#refnote-180)[&#8202;](#ref-back-181-0)[\[179\]](#refnote-181)[&#8202;](#ref-back-182-0)[\[180\]](#refnote-182)[&#8202;](#ref-back-183-0)[\[181\]](#refnote-183)

### Tourism

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Tourism")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Sunset_at_Landmark_Beach.jpg/250px-Sunset_at_Landmark_Beach.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sunset_at_Landmark_Beach.jpg) The Landmark Beach with the urban development [Eko Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eko_Atlantic "Eko Atlantic") in the background

Following the re-modernization project achieved by the previous administration of Governor [Raji Babatunde Fashola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babatunde_Fashola "Babatunde Fashola"), Lagos is gradually becoming a major tourist destination, being one of the largest cities in Africa and the world. Diasporan Africans and others, especially from East and Southern Africa, are increasingly visiting Lagos mostly to understand and experience the Nigeria that has been presented to them by Nollywood.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/The-elegushi-beach-lagos.jpg/250px-The-elegushi-beach-lagos.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The-elegushi-beach-lagos.jpg) [Elegushi Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegushi_Beach "Elegushi Beach")

#### Beaches, water sports

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=32 "Edit section: Beaches, water sports")\]

Lagos has sandy beaches by the Atlantic Ocean, including [Tarkwa Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarkwa_Bay_Beach "Tarkwa Bay Beach"), [Elegushi Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegushi_Beach "Elegushi Beach")[&#8202;](#ref-back-184-0)[\[182\]](#refnote-184) and Alpha Beach. Lagos also has a number of private beaches and resorts, including Box Beach in [Elegushi Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegushi_Beach "Elegushi Beach") and the Inagbe Grand Beach Resort.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TayoAino_-_$100_in_Lagos-0)[\[183\]](#refnote-TayoAino_-_$100_in_Lagos)

On Wole Olateju Crescent, in the immediate vicinity of Lekki Bridge,[&#8202;](#ref-back-186-0)[\[184\]](#refnote-186) there are several companies that offer boat tours and rent kayaks or jet skis.

#### Dancing, shopping, dining

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section: Dancing, shopping, dining")\]

Lagos, especially [Victoria Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Island,_Lagos "Victoria Island, Lagos") and [Surulere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surulere "Surulere"), is said to have "Africa’s craziest nightlife"[&#8202;](#ref-back-187-0)[\[185\]](#refnote-187)[&#8202;](#ref-back-188-0)[\[186\]](#refnote-188)[&#8202;](#ref-back-189-0)[\[187\]](#refnote-189)[&#8202;](#ref-back-TayoAino_-_rooftop_restaurants_in_Lagos-0)[\[188\]](#refnote-TayoAino_-_rooftop_restaurants_in_Lagos) and is likened to [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas "Las Vegas").[&#8202;](#ref-back-191-0)[\[189\]](#refnote-191) Nightclubs are mainly found on [Victoria Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Island,_Lagos "Victoria Island, Lagos"), where the well-to-do and foreign guests hang out, and around [Adeniran Ogunsanya Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adeniran_Ogunsanya_Street,_Lagos "Adeniran Ogunsanya Street, Lagos") in [Surulere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surulere "Surulere").

Rooftop restaurants on the Atlantic beach or on the lagoon offer not only culinary delights and a view but also a breeze from the sea for the visitor.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TayoAino_-_rooftop_restaurants_in_Lagos-1)[\[188\]](#refnote-TayoAino_-_rooftop_restaurants_in_Lagos)

Even before the devaluation of the local currency, the naira, in June 2023, Lagos was a place where US$10 (9.10 euros) could keep you full for a day and buy souvenirs to boot.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TayoAino_-_$100_in_Lagos-1)[\[183\]](#refnote-TayoAino_-_$100_in_Lagos)[&#8202;](#ref-back-192-0)[\[190\]](#refnote-192) The stress-resistant and experienced bargain-hunter buys brand-name clothes for a knockdown price at the eco-market and the adjoining Martin Street.[&#8202;](#ref-back-193-0)[\[191\]](#refnote-193)

The Lekki Arts and Crafts Market (known to Lagosians as Oba Elegushi Market) is a large market that displays a wide variety of African arts and crafts. It is considered the largest art market in Nigeria.[&#8202;](#ref-back-194-0)[\[192\]](#refnote-194)

#### Other tourist attractions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: Other tourist attractions")\]

- [Takwa Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarkwa_Bay_Beach "Tarkwa Bay Beach") – A popular bay from where you can observe shipping traffic in and out of the Lagos port as well as enjoy some water sports. If you have a personal yacht this is where to go.
- [Festac Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festac_Town "Festac Town")[&#8202;](#ref-back-195-0)[\[193\]](#refnote-195)

## Culture

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Culture")\]

Lagos is a cultural centre of Nigeria. As a [port city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port "Port") and the starting point of [British colonisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire "British Empire"), the Western influence is stronger here than in probably any other Nigerian city. All Nigerian ethnic groups can be found in the [melting pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melting_pot "Melting pot") of this [metropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolis "Metropolis"), with the [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people "Yoruba people") predominating. The [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Nigeria "Music of Nigeria") and [film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Nigeria "Cinema of Nigeria") industries in the city are dynamic centres of the country with international acclaim.

### Architecture

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: Architecture")\]

Main article: [Architecture of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Lagos "Architecture of Lagos")

The city of Lagos has the tallest skyline in Nigeria. The architectural styles in Lagos are diverse, ranging from tropical and vernacular to colonial European and ultramodern buildings, or a mixture. Brazilian-style architecture brought by the creoles is evident in buildings such as Water House and Shitta Bey Mosque.[&#8202;](#ref-back-196-0)[\[194\]](#refnote-196)[&#8202;](#ref-back-197-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-197)[&#8202;](#ref-back-198-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-198) Skyscrapers and most high-rise buildings are centered on the islands, while the mainland has some high-rise buildings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-199-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-199)

Lagos adopted a tropical modernist style (a climate-adapted international style), using materials like reinforced concrete, featuring clean lines, louvres, and a sun-screen facade to manage heat and environmental conditions. This style was practised widely by [Onafowokan Michael Olutusen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onafowokan_Michael_Olutusen "Onafowokan Michael Olutusen") and [Oluwole Olumuyiwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oluwole_Olumuyiwa "Oluwole Olumuyiwa") First generation architects in Nigeria. These included paying attention to simplicity rather than ornamentation in the building.

The spatial logic of Lagos between the early 1960s and 1980s followed a clear core periphery model.[&#8202;](#ref-back-200-0)[\[198\]](#refnote-200) Lagos Island, the historic colonial seat, served as the administrative and commercial nucleus, while areas such as Yaba, Apapa, and Surulere developed as peripheral growth zones.[&#8202;](#ref-back-201-0)[\[199\]](#refnote-201) This structure reflected a metabolic approach to city-building in Lagos, where the core’s functions were redistributed outward to manage congestion and promote economic decentralization.[&#8202;](#ref-back-202-0)[\[200\]](#refnote-202) Zoning plans often emphasized the separation of functions, echoing the CIAM-influenced ideals of modernist planning.

### Secular buildings

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Secular buildings")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/National_arts_theatre_%2814981772010%29.jpg/250px-National_arts_theatre_%2814981772010%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:National_arts_theatre_(14981772010).jpg) [National Arts Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Arts_Theatre "National Arts Theatre"), Iganmu, Lagos

The tallest building in Lagos (and in West Africa) is the _NECOM House_ in the Marina commercial centre, the former headquarters of the ex-telecoms monopoly NITEL. The communications mast at the top of the tower also serves as a lighthouse beacon for the Port of Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-203-0)[\[201\]](#refnote-203)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Bookshop_House%2C_Marina.jpg/250px-Bookshop_House%2C_Marina.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bookshop_House,_Marina.jpg) _Bookshop House (2024), statue of [Herbert Macaulay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_Macaulay "Herbert Macaulay")_

A very striking building in Lagos is the _National Arts Theatre_ (officially "Wole Soyinka Center for Culture and Creative Arts") with its oval base, its spaceship-like and military hat form echoing Japan’s Metabolist movement, where structure mirrored function and symbolic nationhood. The renovation of the National Arts Theatre was completed in March 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-204-0)[\[202\]](#refnote-204) With the new "blue line" of the [Lagos light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Rail_Mass_Transit "Lagos Rail Mass Transit"), the National Theatre is recently easily accessible – the station "National theatre" is at a stone's throw distance from the theatre building.

_Tafawa Balewa Square_, near the National Museum, is a stadium with a capacity of 50,000 people, which served as a racecourse under British rule and where independence was proclaimed in 1960. At the entrance to the square stand sculptures of four white horses and seven red eagles.[&#8202;](#ref-back-205-0)[\[203\]](#refnote-205)

The [Oba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oba_of_Lagos "Oba of Lagos")'s (King's) [Palace at Iga Idunganran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iga_Idunganran "Iga Idunganran") is an ancestral palace for the [Oba of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oba_of_Lagos "Oba of Lagos") – the custodian on the traditions and customs of the people of Eko.

Another frequently photographed structure in Lagos is the [Lekki-Ikoyi Link Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lekki-Ikoyi_Link_Bridge "Lekki-Ikoyi Link Bridge") – or more simply: Lekki bridge.

The [Bookshop House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bookshop_House "Bookshop House"). It was built by G. Cappa in 1973 and designed by architects [John Godwin and Gillian Hopwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Godwin_and_Gillian_Hopwood "John Godwin and Gillian Hopwood"). It was designed with special sun screening and windows having a sunbreaker facade, which reduced the heat load on air conditioners by 75% on the office floors.

### Churches and mosques

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section: Churches and mosques")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/The_cathedral_%2816878409017%29.jpg/250px-The_cathedral_%2816878409017%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_cathedral_(16878409017).jpg) The [Cathedral Church of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Church_of_Christ,_Lagos "Cathedral Church of Christ, Lagos") at the central Marina on Lagos Island

The [Cathedral Church of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_Church_of_Christ,_Lagos "Cathedral Church of Christ, Lagos") is the centre of the oldest part of Lagos. It is better known to Lagosians by the acronym CMS (the [Church Missionary Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_Mission_Society "Church Mission Society"), CMS, is the Anglican organisation that runs this church). The Anglican church was built between 1867 and 1869, shortly after the establishment of British colonial rule. The present building, however, was constructed between 1924 and 1946. The remains of the first bishop of African descent in the Anglican Church and translator of the Bible into the Yoruba language, [Samuel Ajayi Crowther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Ajayi_Crowther "Samuel Ajayi Crowther"), were transferred to this cathedral in 1976. A memorial stone commemorates him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-206-0)[\[204\]](#refnote-206)

The _African Church Cathedral Bethel_ is situated at 59 Broad Street. The [African Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_African_Church "The African Church") was founded around 1900 under the leadership of the cocoa farmer (and founder of the first agricultural co-operative in West Africa) Jacob Kehinde Coker as a breakaway from the [Anglican Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglicanism "Anglicanism"), which rejected the use of local musical instruments in worship and the practice of [polygamy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygamy "Polygamy"). It was part of the growing Nigerian sense of self-identity during the early colonial period.[&#8202;](#ref-back-207-0)[\[205\]](#refnote-207) Opposite the cathedral stands the cenotaph in memory of [Taiwo Olowo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwo_Olowo "Taiwo Olowo") (‘Taiwo the Rich’), who is considered one of the founders of Lagos as a financial centre.

The Catholic _Cathedral of the Holy Cross_ on Campbell Street is the heart of the ‘Brazilian quarter’. Following the abolition of [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery "Slavery") in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil "Brazil") in 1888, many former slaves returned to Nigeria and settled in Lagos. These ‘Aguda’ people brought about a significant surge in development in the port city.

The _Church of the Trinity_, part of the [Methodist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodism "Methodism") [Wesleyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesleyan_theology "Wesleyan theology") movement, stands on Tinubu Square. The building replaced the original church, built in 1862, in the 1950s, as it could no longer cope with the influx of worshippers. It was constructed in an architectural style that was considered ‘modern’ in 1950.

The _[Synagogue Church of All Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synagogue_Church_of_All_Nations "Synagogue Church of All Nations")_ was built in 2004. It is part of the Nigerian [Pentecostal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostalism "Pentecostalism") movement, one of Nigeria’s charismatic religious movements.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Alli-Balogun-Mosque-Lagos-Nigeria2.jpg/250px-Alli-Balogun-Mosque-Lagos-Nigeria2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Alli-Balogun-Mosque-Lagos-Nigeria2.jpg) Alli Balogun Mosque

The _[Lagos Central Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Central_Mosque "Lagos Central Mosque")_ on Nnamdi Azikiwe Street in downtown Lagos, with a capacity for 10,000 worshippers, is one of the six largest mosques in Nigeria. It is associated with the (moderate) Jum’at movement. It was inaugurated in 1988 and replaced an older mosque dating from 1905. A few metres further on stands the _Alli Balogun Mosque_, renovated in 2026, which, stylistically, could easily be mistaken for a church. It was built by the shipowner Alli Balogun (a supplier to the German merchants G. L. Gaiser and Bausch&Witt), who was dissatisfied with the Central Mosque. – About 100 metres away is the oldest mosque in Lagos, the _Shitta Bey Mosque_ on Martins Street, which was inaugurated in 1894 and has stood the test of time as a building in the Brazilian Baroque style.

### Art

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Art")\]

Lagos is a hotbed for renowned sculptors, including [Ben Enwonwu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Enwonwu "Ben Enwonwu"), who is internationally renowned for his works in wood and bronze, and the contemporary scrap metal artist [Dotun Popoola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dotun_Popoola "Dotun Popoola"). Other prominent artists include [Peju Alatise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peju_Alatise "Peju Alatise"), Idowu Sonaya (known for his public busts) and experimental artists who are shaping a dynamic, modern art scene, such as Collins Abinoro Akporode and [Omogie Ojei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omogie_Ojei "Omogie Ojei") (femi.the.god), whose multidisciplinary work combines visual art with poetry to document everyday Lagos life.[&#8202;](#ref-back-208-0)[\[206\]](#refnote-208)[&#8202;](#ref-back-209-0)[\[207\]](#refnote-209)[&#8202;](#ref-back-210-0)[\[208\]](#refnote-210)

#### Galleries

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Galleries")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Lagos_Lekki_Nike_Art_Gallery_Aug_2026_IMG_6951b.jpg/250px-Lagos_Lekki_Nike_Art_Gallery_Aug_2026_IMG_6951b.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos_Lekki_Nike_Art_Gallery_Aug_2026_IMG_6951b.jpg) Nike Art Gallery (2026)

The _[Nike Art Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike_Art_Gallery "Nike Art Gallery")_ is an [art gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_gallery "Art gallery") in Lagos owned by [Nike Davies-Okundaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike_Davies-Okundaye "Nike Davies-Okundaye").[&#8202;](#ref-back-211-0)[\[209\]](#refnote-211) The gallery is probably the largest of its kind in West Africa. It is housed in a five-storey building and has a collection of about 8,000 different works of art by various Nigerian artists such as Chief Josephine Oboh Macleod.[&#8202;](#ref-back-212-0)[\[210\]](#refnote-212)

[_Red Door Gallery_](https://www.reddoorgallery.co/) specializes in African contemporary art.

The _Ovuomaroro Studio and Gallery_ is one of the oldest art galleries in Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-213-0)[\[211\]](#refnote-213)

The _Alexis Galleries_, owned by Patty Chidiac-Mastrogiannis, is located on Victoria Island.[&#8202;](#ref-back-214-0)[\[212\]](#refnote-214) Since its foundation in 2011, Alexis Galleries have been engaged in the presentation and dissemination of Nigerian contemporary styles including; painting, drawing, mixed media, and sculpture. It aims to strengthen and support the Nigerian Art Circle.

In 2002, Lagos was one of the African platform cities for the art exhibition _[Documenta 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Documenta11 "Documenta11")_.

#### Public sculptures

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Public sculptures")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Mega-Pipe-Tayo-Olayode-Terfa-Adingi-Lagos-Nigeria-IMG_5195.jpg/250px-Mega-Pipe-Tayo-Olayode-Terfa-Adingi-Lagos-Nigeria-IMG_5195.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mega-Pipe-Tayo-Olayode-Terfa-Adingi-Lagos-Nigeria-IMG_5195.jpg) Sculpture ‘ _MegaPipe_’, in the background the NECOM House

The sculpture “ _MegaPipe_” by Tayo Olayode and Terfa Adingi can be seen on Cable Street, near the NECOM building, Marina underground station and the LAMATA headquarters.[&#8202;](#ref-back-215-0)[\[213\]](#refnote-215)

On 14 Marina Road, in front of the Eko Electricity Distribution Company (EKEDC) building, stands a statue of the [Yoruba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoruba_people "Yoruba people") god Sango. It was created in 1964 by [Ben Enwonwu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Enwonwu "Ben Enwonwu"), arguably Nigeria’s most famous sculptor.

In Yaba, near Sabo Market, Umeh Bede’s sculpture ‘ _Youth Empowerment_’ stands on a traffic island. It depicts eleven books and three people in poses of learning or triumph.[&#8202;](#ref-back-216-0)[\[214\]](#refnote-216)

A roundabout in the Ojodu Berger neighbourhood is not actually a park, as it is not accessible. However, there is a huge black foot there – a sculpture by Hamza Atta.[&#8202;](#ref-back-217-0)[\[215\]](#refnote-217) Consequently, the traffic circle is known as the ‘ _Foot Statue Roundabout_’.[&#8202;](#ref-back-218-0)[\[216\]](#refnote-218)

In the Marwa district, on the edge of a roundabout on the Lekki-Epe expressway, visitors can admire the sculpture ‘ _Virtues of Life_’ by Segun Aiyesan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-219-0)[\[217\]](#refnote-219) It is a cube made of scrap metal, with each side representing a virtue such as ‘love’, ‘faith’ and so on.[&#8202;](#ref-back-220-0)[\[218\]](#refnote-220)

### Museums

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=42 "Edit section: Museums")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Onikan%27s_Archives_%26_Galleries_3.jpg/250px-Onikan%27s_Archives_%26_Galleries_3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Onikan's_Archives_&_Galleries_3.jpg) Interactive exhibition of Yoruba art at the Randle centre

The [_National Museum_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_National_Museum "Nigerian National Museum") in Onikan on Lagos Island houses archaeological and ethnographic collections as well as traditional art. There is an opportunity to purchase Nigerian arts and crafts at the adjoining craft centre. Haggling is allowed at the island's Jankara market. Spices, printed cotton and hand-woven fabrics as well as leather articles are offered here.

The [_John K. Randle Centre_](https://lucas.leeds.ac.uk/research/projects/the-john-k-randle-yoruba-heritage-centre/) houses an exhibition on Yoruba culture inaugurated in 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-221-0)[\[219\]](#refnote-221)[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[220\]](#refnote-222) The curators actively work with artists, writers, craftspeople, historians and storytellers to share the rich Yoruba culture. The John K. Randle Centre is a new, partly interactive kind of museum. It adapts modern Western museum practices to present new forms of storytelling inspired by Yoruba traditions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[221\]](#refnote-223) It celebrates tangible and intangible culture by preserving, enhancing and promoting the cultural heritage of the Yoruba people. The centre actively collects a wide range of items that distinguish it from a traditional museum. The John K. Randle Centre plays a leading role in the repatriation of Yoruba artifacts from European institutions.

The _[National Gallery of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Modern_Art,_Lagos "National Gallery of Modern Art, Lagos")_ (NGMA) is situated within the [National Arts Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre,_Nigeria "National Theatre, Nigeria") and can be accessed via Entrance B. Spread over two floors, it displays colourful abstract paintings by [Bruce Onobrakpeya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Onobrakpeya "Bruce Onobrakpeya") and bronze busts by Ben Osawe. The ‘Portrait Gallery’ section features portraits of prominent figures, including all heads of state. These include works by artists such as [Aina Onabolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aina_Onabolu "Aina Onabolu"), [Hubert Ogunde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Ogunde "Hubert Ogunde"), [Chinua Achebe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinua_Achebe "Chinua Achebe"), [Wole Soyinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wole_Soyinka "Wole Soyinka") and [Ben Enwonwu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Enwonwu "Ben Enwonwu"). Other paintings are by Akinola Lasekan, [Erhabor Emokpae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erhabor_Emokpae "Erhabor Emokpae"), [Solomon Wangboje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon_Wangboje "Solomon Wangboje"), Haig David West and [Gani Odutokun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gani_Odutokun "Gani Odutokun"). There is also a section dedicated to modern sculptures.[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[222\]](#refnote-224)

The _[Kalakuta Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalakuta_Republic "Kalakuta Republic")_ is a museum honouring late musician [Fela Kuti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fela_Kuti "Fela Kuti").[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[223\]](#refnote-225)

Furthermore, the _[Mindscape Children Museum](https://mindscapesmuseums.org/)_ deserves mentioning. Since 2015 it has been notable as Nigeria's premiere Children's museum. It aims at social interaction and improves their natural curiosity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[224\]](#refnote-226)

### Performance

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Performance")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Lagos-Nigeria-MUSON-centre-aug-2026-IMG_6669cut.png/250px-Lagos-Nigeria-MUSON-centre-aug-2026-IMG_6669cut.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos-Nigeria-MUSON-centre-aug-2026-IMG_6669cut.png) Conservatoire MUSON Centre

The [Muson Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muson_Centre "Muson Centre") (Musical Society of Nigeria) is a theatre/performance hall. MUSON regularly organizes concerts of both Nigerian and Western genres. Its choir has performed since 1995, and the symphony orchestra, Nigeria's only professional symphony orchestra at the time, since 2005. Both perform regularly at the annual MUSON Festival and during the Society's concert season.[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-227)

### Festivals

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=44 "Edit section: Festivals")\]

In Lagos, festivals take place in different months. These are the _Lagos Carnival_ in January,[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-228) the _Eko International Film Festival_[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-229) in March, the _Lagos Black Heritage Carnival_ in April,[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-230) the _Lagos Photo Festival_[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-231) in November, the _Book & Art Festival_[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-232) in November and the _Lagos Food Festival_[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-233) in December. The _Lagos Jazz Festival_ features music of all genres with a focus on jazz.[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-234) _The Experience_ is a decibel-rich gospel concert hosted by the [evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism "Evangelicalism") House of the Rock Church in packed [Talewa Balewa Square](#Parks_and_gardens) on the first Friday in December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-235) [Ear protection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearing_protection_device "Hearing protection device") and a certain tolerance for overly dedicated worshippers are recommended.

The _Eyo Carnival_ is an irregular festival that originated in Iperu Remo, Ogun State.[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-236)

### Cuisine

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=45 "Edit section: Cuisine")\]

Main articles: [Nigerian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_cuisine "Nigerian cuisine") and [African cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_cuisine "African cuisine")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Arewa_Traditional_Kitchen.jpg/250px-Arewa_Traditional_Kitchen.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Arewa_Traditional_Kitchen.jpg) Arewa Traditional Kitchen

Some of the famous dishes in Lagos include Indigenous delicacies such as eba and egusi; [amala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amala_(food) "Amala (food)") and [ewedu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ewedu_soup "Ewedu soup"); [jollof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jollof_rice "Jollof rice") (the go-to party dish); [ofada rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofada_rice "Ofada rice"); plantains (locally called dodo); beans; [suya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suya "Suya") (spicy [shish kebab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shish_kebab "Shish kebab") or spiced roasted beef), which is consumed in local clubs and bars with a bottle of cold beer; and [eba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eba "Eba"), made from [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava "Cassava") and eaten with soups prepared with vegetables and mixture of spices and herbs. Other dishes range from local ones like _Iyan_ (pounded yam) made from yam flour, amala; [asaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asaro_(food) "Asaro (food)"), which is usually eaten with various kinds of vegetables; and [Egusi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egusi "Egusi") (melon soup) to European, Middle-Eastern, and Asian cuisine.[&#8202;](#ref-back-post-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-post)[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-238)

### Music

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=46 "Edit section: Music")\]

Lagos is famous throughout Africa for its music scene. Lagos has a vibrant nightlife[&#8202;](#ref-back-nightlife-1)[\[130\]](#refnote-nightlife)[&#8202;](#ref-back-post-1)[\[235\]](#refnote-post)[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-239) and has given birth to a variety of styles such as [Sakara music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakara_music "Sakara music"), [Nigerian hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_hip_hop "Nigerian hip hop"), [highlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highlife "Highlife"), [juju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B9j%C3%BA_music "Jùjú music"), [fuji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuji_music "Fuji music") and [Afrobeats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrobeats "Afrobeats").[&#8202;](#ref-back-MUSIC-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-MUSIC)

[James Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brown "James Brown") performed in Lagos in 1970.[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-241) With his band _[Wings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_McCartney_and_Wings "Paul McCartney and Wings")_, [Paul McCartney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_McCartney "Paul McCartney") recorded his fifth post- [Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatles "Beatles") album, _[Band on the Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band_on_the_Run "Band on the Run")_, in an [EMI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EMI "EMI") studio in Lagos in August and September 1973.[&#8202;](#ref-back-242-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-242) Other foreign musicians who have also performed in the city include [Sean Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Paul "Sean Paul"), [Snoop Dogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snoop_Dogg "Snoop Dogg"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-243) [50 Cent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50_Cent "50 Cent"), [Akon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akon "Akon"), [Jarule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarule "Jarule"), [Ashanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashanti_(singer) "Ashanti (singer)"), [Usher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usher_(singer) "Usher (singer)"), [Shaggy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaggy_(musician) "Shaggy (musician)"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-244) [R Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R_Kelly "R Kelly"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-245) [Cardi B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardi_B "Cardi B"), [Migos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migos "Migos") especially during the [Star Mega Jam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Mega_Jam "Star Mega Jam"); [Shakira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakira "Shakira"), [John Legend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Legend "John Legend"), [Ludacris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludacris "Ludacris"), [Busta Rhymes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Busta_Rhymes "Busta Rhymes"), [Boyz II Men](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyz_II_Men "Boyz II Men"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-246) [T-Pain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Pain "T-Pain"), [Brian McKnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_McKnight "Brian McKnight"), [JayZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JayZ "JayZ"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-247) [Mary J. Blige](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_J._Blige "Mary J. Blige"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-248) [Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9 "Beyoncé"), [Brandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandy_Norwood "Brandy Norwood"), [Ciara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciara "Ciara"), [Keri Hilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keri_Hilson "Keri Hilson") and [Lauryn Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lauryn_Hill "Lauryn Hill").[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-249)[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-250)

### Film

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=47 "Edit section: Film")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Silverbird_Galleria_Cinemas.jpg/250px-Silverbird_Galleria_Cinemas.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Silverbird_Galleria_Cinemas.jpg) [Silverbird Galleria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverbird_Galleria "Silverbird Galleria") cinema in Lagos

The Surulere district is the centre of the Nigerian film industry, commonly referred to as Nollywood. Lagos itself is the location and setting for many films. The city is featured in domestic and foreign feature film productions. Many films are shot in the Festac area of Lagos, which also hosted the World Festival of Black Arts.[&#8202;](#ref-back-NOLLYWOOD-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-NOLLYWOOD) The 2016 film _[Captain America: Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_America:_Civil_War "Captain America: Civil War")_ contains a scene set in Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-252-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-252) The Spanish police series _La unidad_ (2020–2023), the British drama _The last tree_ (2019) and the US-Spanish drama _The Way, Chapter 2_ with [Martin Sheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Sheen "Martin Sheen") (2023 still in development) also use Lagos as a filming location. The film _[93 Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/93_Days "93 Days")_ with [Danny Glover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Glover "Danny Glover") is a somewhat melodramatic but fact-based account of the [Ebola outbreak in Lagos in 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebola_in_Nigeria "Ebola in Nigeria") and was filmed at original locations.

Since the success of the Nigerian thriller _The Figurine_, Nigerian film has increasingly focused on high-quality productions that are also commercially successful. This, in turn, has led to ever-new records in box office takings in Nigeria (2009: "The Figurine", 2013: "Half of a Yellow Sun", 2016: "The Wedding Party").[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-253)

## Sports

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=48 "Edit section: Sports")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Main-bowl-national-stadium-surulere-lagos.jpg/250px-Main-bowl-national-stadium-surulere-lagos.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Main-bowl-national-stadium-surulere-lagos.jpg) [National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stadium,_Lagos "National Stadium, Lagos")

[Association football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football "Association football") is Lagos's most popular sport. Prominent Lagos football clubs include [Bridge Boys F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridge_Boys_F.C. "Bridge Boys F.C."), [MFM F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MFM_F.C. "MFM F.C."), and [First Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Bank_F.C. "First Bank F.C."): both play in [Nigeria National League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_National_League "Nigeria National League"), the second tier of Nigerian football.[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-254)

The [Nigeria national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_national_football_team "Nigeria national football team"), also known as the Super Eagles, used to play almost all of their home games in Lagos at the [National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_National_Stadium "Lagos National Stadium") in Surulere; much later, games were played at the then New [Abuja National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abuja_National_Stadium "Abuja National Stadium") in Abuja for some time; however, games are now mostly played at the newer [Godswill Akpabio International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godswill_Akpabio_International_Stadium "Godswill Akpabio International Stadium") in Uyo, which is the default home of the Super Eagles. Lagos also hosted the 2nd All-African Games in 1973.[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-255)[&#8202;](#ref-back-256-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-256)[&#8202;](#ref-back-257-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-257)

In October 2025 the UIM E1 Lagos GP took place on the Lagos lagoon. It was the first-ever E1 electric powerboat championship race in Africa. Team Brazil won the race.[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-258)

## Education

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=49 "Edit section: Education")\]

See also: [List of schools in Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Lagos "List of schools in Lagos")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Lagos_Business_School%27s_Foyer.jpg/250px-Lagos_Business_School%27s_Foyer.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos_Business_School's_Foyer.jpg) Lagos Business School[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Lagos_Business_School%27s_Cafeteria.jpg/250px-Lagos_Business_School%27s_Cafeteria.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lagos_Business_School's_Cafeteria.jpg) Lagos Business School's Cafeteria

The Lagos State Government operates state schools.[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-259) The education system is the 6-3-3-4 system, which is practiced throughout the country (as well as by many other members of the [Economic Community of West African States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States "Economic Community of West African States")). The levels are Primary, Junior Secondary School (JSS), Senior Secondary School (SSS), and university. All children are offered basic education, with a special focus now on the first nine years. Many of the schools in Nigeria are federally funded and usually are boarding schools. A few examples are the Federal government college Odogbolu (FGCOdogbolu), the Federal government girls' college Sagamu (FGGCSagamu), and the Federal government college Kano (FGCKano). The state of Lagos has its own federally funded high schools namely Federal government college Ijanikin also known as FGC Lagos, King's College Lagos, and Queen's College Lagos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-260)

Lagos is home to postsecondary schools, universities, and other vocational institutions that are either operated by the government or private entities.[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-261)

### Vocational schools

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=50 "Edit section: Vocational schools")\]

- [Institute for Industrial Technology (IIT)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Industrial_Technology_(IIT) "Institute for Industrial Technology (IIT)"): founded in 2000, IIT is a technical [vocational school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_school "Vocational school") for male youth from families with limited resources. Its educational model is based on the [Dual Training System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_education_system "Dual education system").

### Polytechnics

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=51 "Edit section: Polytechnics")\]

- [Yaba College of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaba_College_of_Technology "Yaba College of Technology") (YABATECH): founded in 1934, the college is Nigeria's first higher educational institution and the third in Africa. The college is a centre of culture and heritage. It has student enrolments of over 16,000.
- [Lagos City Polytechnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_City_Polytechnic "Lagos City Polytechnic"), located at 6/8, Bashiru Oweh Street, Off Simbiat Abiola Road (formerly Medical Road), Ikeja – This is the first private Polytechnic in Nigeria. It was established in 1990 by Engr. Babatunde Odufuwa. Engr. Odufuwa hails from Oke-Aye in Ijebu North East Local Government Area (I.N.E.L.G) of Ogun State.
- Grace Polytechnic
- Wolex Polytechnic
- [Federal College of Fisheries and Marine Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_College_of_Fisheries_and_Marine_Technology "Federal College of Fisheries and Marine Technology") is a mono-technic that offers courses in fisheries technology, general science, marine engineering and nautical science.
- Federal College of Education (tech) Akoka
- Ronik Polytechnic[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ronik_Polytechnic_Ejigbo-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-Ronik_Polytechnic_Ejigbo)

### Universities

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lagos&action=edit&section=52 "Edit section: Universities")\]

- The [University of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Lagos "University of Lagos") (UNILAG) Akoka, is a large institution dating from 1962, with over 55,000 students. It comprises 13 faculties, run by over 4,000 staff.[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-263)
- [Lagos State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_University "Lagos State University") (LASU) is a multi-campus university established in the year 1983 and owned by the Lagos State government. The main campus is located at Ojo, along the Lagos-Badagry Expressway.
- [Lagos State University of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_University_of_Science_and_Technology "Lagos State University of Science and Technology") (LASUSTECH) located in Ikorodu was converted from [Lagos State Polytechnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_State_Polytechnic "Lagos State Polytechnic") (LASPOTECH) to a state university of science and technology by Governor Babajide Olusola Sanwoolu leaving Lagos State without a state owned polytechnic.
- [Pan-Atlantic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Atlantic_University "Pan-Atlantic University") formerly known as Pan-African University has a business school ( [LBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos_Business_School "Lagos Business School")), a school of Media and Communication (SMC), and an entrepreneurial development centre (EDC), specialising in providing short courses for SMEs. The School of Media and Communication is also known for its pragmatic communication courses in the fields of journalism, media, and marketing. SMC awards BSc., MSc., and Ph.D. in social science courses. Founded in 1996 and awarded university status in 2002. The university also places some emphasis on the study of [art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art "Art"), running the [Virtual Museum of Modern Nigerian Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_Museum_of_Modern_Nigerian_Art "Virtual Museum of Modern Nigerian Art").
- [National Open University of Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Open_University_of_Nigeria "National Open University of Nigeria") is the first Open university in Nigeria; it is located on Ahmadu Bello Way, Victoria Island, Lagos.
- [Caleb University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caleb_University "Caleb University") is a private university located at Imota, Lagos.
- Lagos State College of Health Technology (LASCOHET) is an institution that runs health courses such as Health Information Management, Pharmacist Tech, Medical Laboratory Tech, Community Health Extension, and Environmental Health Technology; it is located in Yaba.
- [Lagos State University College of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Medicine,_Lagos_State_University "College of Medicine, Lagos State University") (LASUCOM), Ikeja
- [College of Medicine, University of Lagos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Medicine,_University_of_Lagos "College of Medicine, University of Lagos")(CMUL)
  - [![UNILAG, Senate building](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Unilag-Senate-building-IMG_7156cut.jpg/120px-Unilag-Senate-building-IMG_7156cut.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Unilag-Senate-building-IMG_7156cut.jpg "UNILAG, Senate building")
